## Where the Acer loop stands
- **Done:** P0 to P4. These are the retention setting, exporter fixes, routing, the hook outcome log and the design postmortem.
- **Not done:** P5 to P8. The synced vault has no W43 review, no `events-*.jsonl` and no `jarvis-events.js`, and the P5 to P8 Run Records are empty. The Acer may be mid-run, since sync lags.
- **Still open:** P4b (The Plan stays local), P9 to P12, and the owner decisions in the Build Plan.
- **The Dell is held behind a gate:** the Acer loop must run end to end, a written recipe must exist, and no hand copies are allowed.

## Things you should know now
1. **The Jarvis repo is PUBLIC.** I checked with `gh`. The Acer notes recorded visibility as "not checked", and the sync reference says "public" in passing. Session notes embed tool results, and 46 files of The Plan's sessions are tracked and pushed. That is more urgent than P4b's framing suggests, and rewriting history is your call.
2. **Dell transcripts start expiring around 2026-11-05.** Two are from 10-06, one from 10-07, and then roughly one a day. The Dell needs its P0 (retention plus a snapshot) before it needs anything else. The WSL transcripts older than 30 days will go at the next WSL Claude session start.
3. **The Dell's `The Plan` notes are unprotected.** There are 2 notes under `Dell/Windows/Claude/The Plan/`. Syncthing carries them to the Acer, whose git then pushes them. The P4b ignore lines will have to cover the Dell's path too.
4. **`Jarvis-WeeklyReview` fires on both laptops.** The 11-01 duplicate fire is logged. A scheduled digest could do the same.
5. **The Dell's `Jarvis-Syncthing-Health` is currently alerting.** The banner reads "not running" at 22:56, yet port 8384 answers 200 and two Syncthing processes are up. The failure counter was at 625 on 11-01 with no established cause.
6. **The PDF guard has nothing to call on the Dell.** If you register it without MinerU, the guard sends Claude to `mineru-pdf.ps1`, which cannot work.

## Web research
- **Hooks reference, verified today:** still 33 events. `UserPromptSubmit` fires for typed prompts, `/loop`, background-subagent reports and messages from other sessions, but the page is silent on built-ins like `/clear`. Async hooks are killed under `-p`.
- **Hooks reference, Windows details:** `if` is evaluated only on tool events, and hooks are held back until workspace trust is accepted. A bare `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` resolves to null in PowerShell, so `$env:` is required.
- **Retention:** `usage-data/` is swept and `history.jsonl` is kept. The sweep runs in the background after a session starts.
- **Changelog 2.1.307 to 2.1.318:**
  - `SubagentStop` fired about twice a minute before 2.1.311.
  - `jq` could not parse transcripts cut through an emoji before 2.1.309, so parsers need to tolerate bad lines.
  - `claude -p` could exit 0 while losing its transcript before 2.1.317.
  - Nested `claude -p` reuses the parent session's ID unless `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NESTED_SESSION_ID=1`.
  - PowerShell hooks only honor exit code 2 reliably from 2.1.316.
  - The 2.1.318 entry is dated November 4, which is suspect.
- **Sonnet 5.5 guide:** it adds nothing on XML structure. The directives to carry into prompts are the carry-through, stop-and-report and real-check paragraphs the Acer prompts already use. Effort should be `medium` for specified tasks and `high` for harder ones, with `xhigh` and `max` reserved until measured.
- **Anthropic judgment-calls study (Oct 27, 50 analysts):** analysts matched the agent's lean 86% of the time, and 77% repeated its argument. This directly challenges stage A of the second reviewer, so its flags must stay hidden until after the human's note.
- **Husain and Shankar** (modified 2026-09-01) and the **weekly setup review** (Oct 7, one observed run) both match what the Sources note already records.

## What the Dell prompts will have to handle
- **Path portability:** the vault and home paths differ, so every path must resolve from the script's own location, with no literals.
- **Per-machine state:** registration must be done by a vault script run on the Dell. Syncing a script file registers nothing.
- **Local ignore edits:** `.stignore` needs `(?d)` lines applied by hand on the Dell.
- **Phase order:** Dell retention and snapshot, a Dell-side `settings.json` reconciliation, then event hook, digest and schedule. Repo-only scope applies, and WSL and Cowork stay separate decisions.
- **The format conflict:** the Acer prompts use a role, context, goal, steps, report layout, but the Agent Prompt Standard requires four other headings.
- **Process rules from my saved memory:**
  - Infra goes in small builds run in fresh sessions.
  - Global `settings.json` edits need your explicit ask in that turn.
  - Don't repeat a fenced prompt in chat.

## Not covered
- **Web sources:** the pages came via a subagent, and some long-page parts were model summaries rather than verbatim text. I flagged those.
- **Pages left unread:** the Acer's per-source papers (Tang, Buçinca, AgenTracer and the rest) were taken as the Sources note states them. I did not re-open the monitoring-usage page.
- **Dell scans:** I did not open `history.jsonl` or the credential files, only line counts. I did not scan the Dell's full note corpus or run the coverage script. A full-vault `find` I started is still running in the background and is not needed.

## Decision for you
Do the Dell prompts wait on P5 to P8 reporting, or should I design the Dell retention and snapshot prompt first? It needs nothing from the Acer and the transcripts are expiring.

== CLAUDE.md
# Global Claude Code Instructions

@AGENTS.md

## Writing style

- Never use an em dash (—). Use a plain hyphen (-) instead, or restructure the sentence. Applies to all output, in every project.

## Terminal response formatting

Applies to all output, in every project. The terminal (nerd font, Tokyo Night/One Half Dark theme) and Claude Code's own markdown renderer control actual colors - a message can't pick arbitrary RGB values. What's actually controllable is structure, so use it deliberately instead of writing flat paragraphs:

- Use headers (`##`/`###`) to break up any response with more than one distinct part, not just long ones.
- Use **bold** for the one or two things in a response that matter most - the answer, the blocker, the thing that changed. Don't bold everything; that erases the signal.
- Use `code spans` for every literal command, path, flag, or config value - anything the user might copy. This is what actually gets syntax-highlighted and made easy to grab in this setup, so it's the real substitute for "highlight the copyable stuff."
- Use fenced code blocks (with a language tag) for anything longer than one line that's meant to be run or pasted verbatim.
- Use *italics* sparingly, for caveats, uncertainty, or an aside - not for emphasis (that's bold's job).
- Don't stack all of these in every message. A one-line answer stays a one-line answer.

## Jarvis vault

<!-- Placeholder - expand this section in a later round; see Prompts.md's next round for the full framing. -->
The Jarvis vault (`D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis`) is this machine's central knowledge base. In Anant's own words: "Jarvis is the main point for the entire laptop... we are using Jarvis for literally each and everything." Treat it as the default target for anything vault-shaped until this section is expanded.

cd ~/.claude/hooks && echo "=== after-edit-log.ps1"; cat after-edit-log.ps1; echo; echo "=== session-wrapup.ps1"; cat session-wrapup.ps1; echo; echo "=== activity log (counts only)"; f=~/.claude/jarvis-session-activity.jsonl; wc -l $f; head -c 3 $f | xxd | head -1; python - <<'EOF'
import json,collections,os
f=os.path.expanduser('~/.claude/jarvis-session-activity.jsonl')
c=collections.Counter(); first=last=None
for line in open(f,encoding='utf-8-sig',errors='replace'):
    try:
        d=json.loads(line)
    except Exception: c['bad']+=1; continue
    c[d.get('event')]+=1
    ts=d.get('timestamp') or d.get('ts')
    first=first or ts; last=ts
print(dict(c)); print(first,last)
EOF
```
```
=== after-edit-log.ps1
# after-edit-log.ps1
# Logs every file edit to ~/.claude/session-logs/<today>-edits.md
# PostToolUse hook - must NEVER exit non-zero, it is logging-only
# ASCII-only file: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (powershell.exe) reads BOM-less
# .ps1 files using the system codepage, not UTF-8, so any non-ASCII
# character here (em dashes included) can corrupt into a stray quote and
# cause a parse-time syntax error that no try/catch in this file can catch.

$ErrorActionPreference = "SilentlyContinue"

try {
    $today     = Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd"
    $timestamp = Get-Date -Format "HH:mm:ss"

    $filePath = $env:CLAUDE_FILE_PATHS
    if (-not $filePath) {
        $raw = [Console]::In.ReadToEnd()
        if ($raw) {
            try {
                $hook = $raw | ConvertFrom-Json
                $filePath = $hook.tool_input.file_path
                if (-not $filePath) { $filePath = $hook.tool_input.path }
            } catch {}
        }
    }
    if (-not $filePath) { $filePath = "(unknown)" }

    # Native Windows pwsh doesn't always populate $env:HOME the way WSL's
    # pwsh does - fall back to $env:USERPROFILE (this repo's WSL original
    # doesn't need this fallback, so it's Windows-only, not backported).
    $homeDir = $env:HOME
    if (-not $homeDir) { $homeDir = $env:USERPROFILE }
    $logDir = "$homeDir/.claude/session-logs"
    if (-not (Test-Path $logDir)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $logDir -Force | Out-Null
    }

    $logFile = "$logDir/$today-edits.md"
    $entry   = "- **$timestamp** ``$filePath``"
    Add-Content -Path $logFile -Value $entry -Encoding UTF8
} catch {
    # Silently swallow all errors - never block a turn
}

exit 0

=== session-wrapup.ps1
# session-wrapup.ps1
# Prints a one-line reminder to log the session if no log exists for today
# Stop hook - must NEVER exit non-zero
# ASCII-only file: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (powershell.exe) reads BOM-less
# .ps1 files using the system codepage, not UTF-8, so any non-ASCII
# character here (em dashes included) can corrupt into a stray quote and
# cause a parse-time syntax error that no try/catch in this file can catch.

$ErrorActionPreference = "SilentlyContinue"

try {
    [Console]::OutputEncoding = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8
    $today   = Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd"
    # Native Windows pwsh doesn't always populate $env:HOME - fall back to
    # $env:USERPROFILE (Windows-only fallback, not backported to the WSL
    # original since WSL's pwsh always sets $env:HOME correctly).
    $homeDir = $env:HOME
    if (-not $homeDir) { $homeDir = $env:USERPROFILE }
    $logDir  = "$homeDir/.claude/session-logs"
    $logFile = "$logDir/$today-session.md"

    if (-not (Test-Path $logFile)) {
        Write-Host "[ session ] No log for $today - run /second-brain-compress or vault /closeday when done."
    }
} catch {
    # Silently swallow all errors
}

exit 0

    foreach ($a in $t.Actions) { "  EXEC: $($a.Execute)  ARGS: $($a.Arguments)  WD: $($a.WorkingDirectory)" }
    foreach ($tr in $t.Triggers) { "  TRIG: $($tr.CimClass.CimClassName) start=$($tr.StartBoundary) rep=$($tr.Repetition.Interval) dur=$($tr.Repetition.Duration) daysOfWeek=$($tr.DaysOfWeek)" }
    "  Settings: StartWhenAvail=$($t.Settings.StartWhenAvailable) Battery=$($t.Settings.DisallowStartIfOnBatteries) Hidden=$($t.Settings.Hidden) Limit=$($t.Settings.ExecutionTimeLimit)"
  }
}

First match wins, so put narrower paths above wider ones. project is the folder name under the machine's Claude folder. match is 'under' (the path itself or anything inside it) or 'exact' (only that path). path may use {vault}, {root}, {home} and {localappdata}, and may use \\..\\ to step up. Directories no rule matches get a folder named after the directory (fallback 'leaf'). == backfill-windows-silent.vbs
' Silent launcher for ConversationCapture-Backfill-Windows.
' WindowStyle 0 = hidden. Synchronous wait (waitOnReturn=True) + honest exit
' code, same corrected pattern as sync-all-silent.vbs (2026-08-11). This is
' a safety-net catch-up for anything the SessionEnd/Stop hooks missed - the
' hooks are the primary capture mechanism, this just closes the gap when a
' hook doesn't fire (crash, forced close, machine sleep mid-session).
Option Explicit
Dim sh, exitCode, scriptDir, exportScript, healthScript
Set sh = CreateObject("WScript.Shell")
scriptDir = CreateObject("Scripting.FileSystemObject").GetParentFolderName(WScript.ScriptFullName)
exportScript = scriptDir & "\export-claude-session.ps1"
healthScript = scriptDir & "\update-capture-health.ps1"
exitCode = sh.Run("powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File """ & exportScript & """ -BackfillAll", 0, True)
' Records this run's honest exit code to the Capture Health dashboard note
' (added 2026-08-19 - reliability gap fix). Runs after the real work so it
' can never mask exitCode below; ignore its own exit status.
sh.Run "powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File """ & healthScript & """ -TaskLabel Windows -ExitCode " & exitCode, 0, True
WScript.Quit(exitCode)
```

.obsidian/workspaces.json

// Permanently disabled plugins (2026-10-09, corrected 2026-10-11) - same
// reasoning as .gitignore's entry. Moved out of .obsidian/plugins/ entirely
// (not just renamed in place) after discovering on 2026-10-11 that a renamed
// in-place folder still loads: Obsidian indexes plugin manifests by the `id`
// field inside manifest.json, not the folder's name, so lazy-plugins' own
// stale tracking entries were enough to re-trigger a load regardless of the
// folder's name. Local-only disable backups, never meant to sync.
.obsidian/_disabled-plugins/
// (?d)-prefixed (Build 13, 2026-10-06): each of these is one file inside a
// plugin folder that otherwise holds normally-synced content (main.js,
// manifest.json, styles.css) - if that plugin is ever uninstalled on one
// machine (folder deleted) while this file still exists locally on the
// other, Syncthing hits the identical "contains ignored files" deadlock
// found live on cursor-workflow/logs/ this build. See that block below for
// the full incident.
(?d).obsidian/plugins/copilot/data.json
(?d).obsidian/plugins/copilot/data-*backup*.json

// Copilot plugin's model-catalog cache (2026-10-07) - a 5MB+ regenerated blob,
// refreshed independently on each laptop, found live generating a real
// .sync-conflict-* on this exact file. (?d)-prefixed per Failure Mode 17: this
// is the ONLY file in .copilot/, so excluding it makes that directory 100%
// ignored content - exactly the shape that caused the cursor-workflow/logs/
// delete-dir deadlock.
(?d).copilot/model-catalog-cache.json

// community-plugins.json and lazy-plugins' own data.json (2026-10-08) - same
// reasoning as .gitignore's entry: lazy-plugins actively rewrites
// community-plugins.json as part of normal operation (temporarily removing a
// plugin, re-adding it after a delay), and Syncthing's real-time watcher has
// no way to tell that apart from a real, intended change - it would propagate
// whatever transient mid-cycle state it catches to the other laptop
// instantly, same corruption risk as the git side, just faster. Both excluded
// here too - per-machine plugin-enablement state, not shared content.
.obsidian/community-plugins.json
.obsidian/plugins/lazy-plugins/data.json
(?d).obsidian/plugins/quickadd/data.json
(?d).obsidian/plugins/obsidian-local-rest-api/data.json
(?d).obsidian/plugins/lean-terminal/data.json
(?d).obsidian/plugins/recent-files-obsidian/data.json
(?d).obsidian/plugins/file-explorer-plus/data.json
(?d).obsidian/copilot-index-*.json
30_Order/System/jarvis-memory/*.sqlite*
// (?d)-prefixed (Build 14, 2026-10-09): these two patterns were the one spot
// in the AI Conversations tree that never got the (?d) fix applied in Build
// 13, even though it's the identical "contains ignored files" delete deadlock.
// Session folders deleted upstream (CausalOps, everything-claude-code,
// hivemind, _archive-pre-fix*, ~50 paths total) each still held a _raw_jsonl/
// or _raw_composer/ folder of ignored .jsonl content, so the delete could
// never complete here. Confirmed live: 13,038 "Failed to delete directory"
// retries since Build 13 landed (2026-10-06 12:32) plus 1,155 more on
// 2026-10-09 alone, retried every ~16 min, the sole cause of "out of sync".
(?d)60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/**/_raw_jsonl

import os,glob,datetime,collections
root=os.path.expanduser('C:/Users/Anant Gupta/.claude/projects')
rows=[]
for p in glob.glob(root+'/*/*.jsonl'):
    st=os.stat(p); rows.append((datetime.datetime.fromtimestamp(st.st_mtime),st.st_size,p))
rows.sort()
now=datetime.datetime(2026,11,3,22,50)
print("top-level transcripts:",len(rows),"total MB:",round(sum(r[1] for r in rows)/1e6,1))
byday=collections.Counter(r[0].strftime('%Y-%m-%d') for r in rows)
for d in sorted(byday): 
    age=(now-datetime.datetime.strptime(d,'%Y-%m-%d')).days
    print(d,byday[d],"age_days~",age, "<-- expires within 3 days at 30d default" if age>=27 else "")
print("largest 3:",[ (round(r[1]/1e6,1),os.path.basename(os.path.dirname(r[2]))[-20:]) for r in sorted(rows,key=lambda r:-r[1])[:3]])
EOF
echo; echo "== history.jsonl line count only"; wc -l "C:/Users/Anant Gupta/.claude/history.jsonl"

// Every number here is read from today's daily note. Nothing is stored on this page.
const DAILY = "10_Areas/Life/Enumerate/Daily";
const todayStr = dv.date("today").toFormat("yyyy-MM-dd");
const p = dv.page(`${DAILY}/${todayStr}`);
const count = (keys) => (p ? keys.filter((k) => p[k] === true).length : 0);
const lc = Number(p?.lc_count ?? 0);
const study = Number(p?.study_today ?? 0);
const wins = count(["win_project", "win_career", "win_cleanup", "win_review"]);
const floor = count(["floor_1", "floor_2", "floor_3"]);
const open = dv.pages(`"${DAILY}"`).file.tasks
  .where((t) => !t.checked && String(t.section?.subpath ?? t.section).includes("Daily Loses")).length;
const lcClass = lc >= 5 ? 'green' : lc > 0 ? 'yellow' : '';
const winsClass = wins >= 4 ? 'green' : wins >= 2 ? 'yellow' : '';
const floorClass = floor >= 3 ? 'green' : floor >= 1 ? 'yellow' : '';
const losesClass = open === 0 ? 'green' : open > 3 ? 'red' : 'yellow';
const container = dv.el('div', '', {cls: 'stat-grid'});
container.innerHTML = `
  <div class="stat-tile ${winsClass}">
    <div class="stat-label">Wins</div>
    <div class="stat-value">${wins}<span style="font-size:0.85rem;opacity:0.6">/4</span></div>
    <div class="stat-delta ${wins >= 4 ? 'positive' : wins >= 2 ? 'neutral' : 'negative'}">${wins >= 4 ? 'all four' : 'toggles ticked'}</div>
  </div>
  <div class="stat-tile ${floorClass}">
    <div class="stat-label">Floor</div>
    <div class="stat-value">${floor}<span style="font-size:0.85rem;opacity:0.6">/3</span></div>
    <div class="stat-delta ${floor >= 3 ? 'positive' : floor >= 1 ? 'neutral' : 'negative'}">${floor >= 3 ? 'floor held' : 'toggles ticked'}</div>
  </div>
  <div class="stat-tile ${lcClass}">
    <div class="stat-label">Problems</div>
    <div class="stat-value">${lc}</div>
    <div class="stat-delta ${lc >= 5 ? 'positive' : 'neutral'}">target 5</div>
  </div>
  <div class="stat-tile">
    <div class="stat-label">Study Hours</div>
    <div class="stat-value">${study}h</div>
    <div class="stat-delta neutral">today</div>
  </div>
  <div class="stat-tile ${losesClass}">
    <div class="stat-label">Open Loses</div>
    <div class="stat-value">${open}</div>
    <div class="stat-delta ${open === 0 ? 'positive' : 'negative'}">${open === 0 ? 'none slipped' : 'not made up'}</div>
  </div>

23:# Cross-Laptop Sync - Known Failure Modes and Prevention
25:## Why This Note Exists
27:## Gate
29:## Steps
31:### Failure Mode 1: Platform-Incompatible Paths
37:### Failure Mode 2: Reserved/Invalid Filenames
43:### Failure Mode 3: Secrets Committed Or Left On Disk
51:### Failure Mode 4: A Safety-Net Setting Silently Reverted Or Got Disabled
59:### Failure Mode 5: Two Uncoordinated Writers On The Same Files (the main one)
66:### Failure Mode 6: A Git Rebase Can Silently Pick The Stale Side As Canonical
81:### Failure Mode 7: The `obsidian-kanban` Plugin Rewrites Full Files On Interaction
87:### Failure Mode 8: The Health-Check Script Itself Counted Its Own Safety Net As A Problem
95:### Failure Mode 9: `file-explorer-plus/data.json` Is Pure UI State That Never Needed To Sync
102:### Failure Mode 10: The Same Stale-Path Bug Was Baked Into 15 Scripts, Not Just The Sync Ones
110:### Failure Mode 11: `Jarvis-WeeklyReview` Was Documented As Fixed Twice And Never Actually Existed
115:### Failure Mode 12: A Correctly-Running Safety Net Has No Path To A Human
123:### Failure Mode 13: A Syncthing-Side Exclusion Was Never Mirrored To Git's Own List
131:### Failure Mode 14: A Vault-Synced Script Fix Doesn't Retroactively Fix An Already-Registered Scheduled Task
137:### Failure Mode 15: A Live Alert Written Into A Shared File Is A Permanent, By-Design Conflict Source
145:### Failure Mode 16: The Same Stale-Path Bug Recurred, In 14 Files, After Being "Fixed" Once Already
150:### Failure Mode 17: Ignoring A File Isn't Enough If Its Directory Can Become Empty
159:### Failure Mode 18: A Logon-Triggered Scheduled Task Doesn't Reliably Fire On Laptop Sleep/Resume
165:### Failure Mode 19: `git-auto-sync.ps1`'s Own `git add -A` Raced An Active Obsidian Save
170:### Failure Mode 20: Two Independent Git Writers Was The Root Cause, Not Simultaneous Editing
180:### Failure Mode 21: The Conversation Exporter Wrote One Session Into Two Folders And Counted Every Response Two To Three Times
185:### How To Verify Sync Is Actually Healthy Right Now (Not Just "Looks Fine")

# Review System
==Every review layer cites the real rows it read, fixes only what it is certain about, and hands anything unresolved to the layer above it.==
This is the map and shared contract for how reviews nest in this vault. It does not say what any single review contains; each layer has its own Standard for that. Read it to learn which review applies, where its output goes, and how a finding escalates.
## Review layers
| Layer | Cadence | What it reviews | Standard or skill | Output goes to |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Daily | each working day | the day's plan and scorecard | `/startday`, `/closeday`, [[Plan Standard]] | the daily note in `10_Areas/Life/Enumerate/Daily/` |
| Project | weekly or monthly per project | one project note's truth and stall state | [[Per Project Review]] | the project note's own `## Log` |
| Plan | each review cycle | a `type: plan` note's progress | [[Plan Review Cadence]] | the plan's Current Progress section |
| Internship loop | weekly and monthly | the internship pipeline's output | [[Internship Loop Review Standard]] | `60_Claude/30_Reviews/Internship Loop/` |
| AI tools | weekly and monthly | how Claude Code, Cowork, and sync are used | [[Review Standard]] | `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Scheduled/` |
| Vault | weekly | build, enrichment, and structural health | `/weekly-review`, [[Capability Review Standard]] | `60_Claude/30_Reviews/Weekly Synthesis/` |
| Audit | on demand | one system or pipeline, or a change set | [[Audit Report Standard]] | `60_Claude/30_Reviews/` |
| Ops | on demand | link, frontmatter, and orphan health | `/ops health-check`, agent `vault-curator` | `60_Claude/30_Reviews/Ops Reports/` |
## Rules every layer shares
- **Cite real rows.** Every claim traces to a file, count, or log row opened this pass. A source not opened is listed as not reviewed.
- **Reviews do not fix.** A review that surfaces a problem is not authorization to auto-fix it. Decided Fixes holds only items with 100% clarity, applied by hand or flagged for the next build session.
- **Open Questions carry.** They are never deleted without being resolved.
- **Carryover Escalation.** A finding or open question that shows up in the same slot twice in a row is promoted to Immediate Actions in the next review. Do not carry it a third time unremarked.
- **Honest emptiness.** "Nothing to report" is a valid finding. Do not invent one to fill a heading.
- **Nothing runs on its own.** Every review is triggered by the owner or an agent asked to run it. A review not run is a gap to record, not to hide.
## How findings escalate
A project review's unresolved items surface in the vault's weekly review. A weekly item still open at month end is carried by the monthly review of its own layer. An item that reaches a second monthly sighting is a discipline gap and goes to the owner as a decision, not another note.
> [!WARNING]
> Adding a new review layer because a topic feels important. Check this table first. If an existing layer covers the subject, extend its Standard. A new layer needs a named trigger and a named owner, the test [[Log Standard]] applies to logs.
## Done Conditions
- The review run was matched to a row in the table above.
- Its output landed where the table says.
- Anything two reviews old was escalated.
## Gold Standard Example
None for the system as a whole. For one layer, see the Gold Standard Example in [[Review Standard]].

# Dell Windows Track
## Summary
Read from the Acer through synced notes only; the Dell was not reached and nothing about its live hook or tasks was checked. The track holds the oldest and largest Claude Code archive: 179 notes from 2026-06-04 to 2026-10-18, plus 16 Cursor notes that stopped in July. It is the source of the 107-note duplicate problem, because the 10-08 reorganization copied the older `Windows/` trees into it and left them in place.
## Scope
`Dell/Windows/Claude/` and `Dell/Windows/Cursor/` as they appear in the Acer's copy of the vault on 2026-10-21. Live state of the Dell is unverified. Registration facts are from Build 5c's 2026-10-17 session, listed in [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration|Capture Registration]].

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Claude notes | 179: Jarvis 104, Cowork 64, Home 10, The Plan 1 |
| Date range | 2026-06-04 to 2026-10-18 (session start) |
| Tokens and cost | about 6.16 billion tokens and $3,110 as written in the notes; both are inflated roughly 2 to 3 times by duplicate counting (see [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Export Conformance\|Export Conformance]]) and undercounted for unpriced newer models |
| September and October | 35 notes ($711) and 9 notes ($212) |
| Cowork | 64 notes from 2026-06-04 to 2026-07-24, none since |
| Cursor | 16 notes (`Jarvis` 12, `hackathon` 3, `The Plan` 1), 2026-05-28 to 2026-07-05; folders last touched 2026-10-08 |
| Archive folders | `Claude/_archive-pre-fix` 23, `Claude/Cowork/_archive-pre-fix` 63, `Cursor/_archive-pre-fix` 3 |
| Tool log | `Dell/Windows/Claude/Tool log.md`, 0 bytes |
## Findings
### 1. The track holds copies of the legacy trees
All 63 Cowork sessions and all 34 legacy Claude Code Jarvis sessions in `Windows/` also sit here. That is 97 of the 107 duplicate notes (the rest come from the same session appearing in two project folders). Deleting the legacy tree is safe for those 97; 8 legacy Home and The Plan notes have no copy.
### 2. Cowork stopped on 2026-07-24 and nothing has noticed
No Cowork month folder exists after `2026-07`. The Cowork sweep runs from the `SessionEnd` hook, so either the hook stopped running on the Dell for a time, Cowork was not used, or the sweep path broke. The 10-04 audit recorded it as dead; it was never diagnosed.
### 3. Session starts end at 2026-10-18
The Dell's last Claude Code session in this track started on 2026-10-18. Notes were touched later (the Jarvis folder shows 2026-10-21 modification times), which fits re-export of a growing session or a sync, not a new session. Whether the Dell has run Claude Code on Windows since 10-18 is unknown from here.
### 4. Four Session Index files query the wrong folder
`Dell/Windows/Claude/Unknown` and the three Cursor project folders point at the old `Windows/...` roots.
### 5. Cursor capture is a Dell-only mechanism that has stalled
The sweep task `Jarvis-Cursor-Session-Export` runs on the Dell. Cursor notes here end in July, and the Cursor folders were last written 2026-10-08.
## Priority Queue
1. When the Dell is next reachable, run the checks in [[60_Claude/07_AI_Information/How to Use Claude/Track Conversations|Track Conversations]] there and fill the unverified rows in Capture Registration. Answers findings 2, 3, and 5.
2. Decide the legacy `Windows/` trees. Answers finding 1. Owner decision.
3. Diagnose the Cowork stop before wiring or retiring Cowork. Answers finding 2.
## Open Questions
- [ ] Is the Windows hook on the Dell still registered, and did Claude Code run there after 2026-10-18?
- [ ] Is `Jarvis-Cursor-Session-Export` still registered and enabled?
- [ ] Is the 2026-10-17 Build 5c state (shared scripts, machine lookup) in place on the Dell's Windows side, or only its WSL side?

# Agent Prompt Standard
==A prompt note holds one task for a fresh agent session and everything that session needs, so the run does not depend on what the writer remembers.==
This is the standard for notes that hold a prompt to paste into Claude Code, Codex, or another agent: the numbered CausalOps prompts and the Internship Building System runs. It covers the prompt note and, in a second Spec, the rolling queue file that holds only the next prompt. The plan a prompt belongs to is a [[Spec and Plan Standard]] note.
## Spec
```json
{
  "family": "agent-prompt",
  "tier": 1,
  "match": {"type": "project", "kind": "prompt", "path": "^(20_Progress|60_Claude/40_Project_Briefs)/", "where": {}, "where_not": {"prompt_shape": "queue"}},
  "legacy_match": [
    {"path": "^60_Claude/40_Project_Briefs/CausalOps/claude code/"},
    {"type": "project", "path": "^20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Runs/Prompt "}
  ],
  "status": ["active", "paused", "complete", "archived"],
  "required_keys": ["type", "status", "created", "updated", "tags", "project_kind", "task", "model"],
  "key_types": {"created": "date", "updated": "date", "tags": "list"},
  "headings": ["Why This Exists", "Hard Rules", "Implementation Order", "Report Back"],
  "optional_headings": ["What Not To Do", "Status", "Related Notes"]
}
```
```json
{
  "family": "prompt-queue",
  "tier": 1,
  "match": {"type": "project", "kind": "prompt", "path": "^20_Progress/", "where": {"prompt_shape": "queue"}, "where_not": {}},
  "legacy_match": [{"type": "project", "path": "^20_Progress/Internship/Building System/Runs/(Claude Code|Codex) Prompts( - Archive)?\\.md"}],
  "status": ["active", "paused", "complete", "archived"],
  "required_keys": ["type", "status", "created", "updated", "tags", "project_kind", "prompt_shape", "next"],
  "key_types": {"created": "date", "updated": "date", "tags": "list"},
  "headings": ["Prompting Guide In Use"],
  "optional_headings": []
}
```
## Headings
### Frontmatter
`project_kind: prompt`. `task` is a short slug of the job, for example `mcp-server-port-fix`. `model` is the agent and model it was written for. `status` is `active` until it runs, `complete` after the result is reviewed, `archived` once superseded. `prompt_shape: queue` marks the rolling file; it is omitted on a single prompt.
### Why This Exists
The evidence behind the task: the finding, with a file path, commit, or review reference. A hypothesis the note wrote itself is labeled as one.
> [!WARNING]
> Handing the agent a diagnosis that was never checked. State what was verified and what is assumed, and tell the agent to re-check it.
### Hard Rules
Scope limits and constraints, one line each: what to leave untouched, what must stay green, what is out of scope this round.
### Implementation Order
Numbered steps with the files touched, in the order to do them.
### Report Back
What the agent must report so the result can be reviewed: files changed, commands run with output, anything it could not do. A prompt without this gets a report nobody can check.
### What Not To Do
Optional. The two or three mistakes a past run made.
### Queue file
The queue holds only the next prompt or two, under `Prompting Guide In Use` and one `##` per prompt, and is rewritten each cycle ([[Prompt Cycle]]).
## Legacy Shapes
| Old shape | New shape | Move |
|---|---|---|
| No `project_kind`, `task`, or `model` (the CausalOps `ran/` prompts have `tags`, `created`, sometimes `task`, `model`) | Add all three | Mechanical once the model is known |
| Prompt without Report Back or Hard Rules | Add the missing section from the prompt text | Judgment |
| `status: active` on a prompt that already ran | `complete` | Judgment |
| Rolling `Claude Code Prompts` or `Codex Prompts` file | Add `prompt_shape: queue`; archive files get `status: archived` | Mechanical |
| A scratch ledger from one run (Freshness Sweep Scratch) | Not a prompt: keep as `project_kind: plan` with `status: complete`, or drop it | Judgment |
## Done Conditions
- A prompt note has the four required headings, `task`, and `model`.
- Every claim in Why This Exists says whether it was verified and how.
- A finished prompt moves to `status: complete` or to the Archive note, with its result linked.
- The note passes [[Vault Lint]] for `agent-prompt` or `prompt-queue`.
## Gold Standard Example
[[60_Claude/40_Project_Briefs/CausalOps/claude code/ran/01 - Fix MCP Server Deployment and Housekeeping|Prompt 1: Fix the MCP Server Deployment]]: a cited finding, hard rules, an ordered implementation, and a report-back section. It predates `project_kind`, `status`, and `updated`, which Legacy Shapes adds.

---
type: system
status: sprout

  - "[[Spec and Plan Standard]]"

# Prompt Cycle
Write the next prompt, run it, review the result against the repo, and archive it, so the queue only ever holds what runs next.
## Use when
A project like the internship research loop or CausalOps has a round of agent work to run. The Building System runs are the worked example.
## Steps
1. Open the queue note (`Claude Code Prompts`, `Codex Prompts`). It holds the next prompt and only that.
2. Check the evidence for Why This Exists against the live repo. A hypothesis the queue wrote itself is labeled, and said plainly when it turns out wrong.
3. Write the prompt from [[Agent Prompt Template]] with scope in Hard Rules and a Report Back section.
4. Run it in a fresh agent session.
5. Review Report Back against the repo: the commands and their output, not the agent's summary.
6. Move the finished prompt and its reviewed result to the Archive note and set its `status` to `complete`.
7. Rewrite the queue with the next prompt, and add a Log entry to the project note.
## Do not
Do not let finished prompts pile up in the queue, hand over an unchecked diagnosis, or accept a report you did not compare to the repo.
## Done when
- The queue holds only the next prompt.
- The finished prompt and its result are in the Archive note, with `status: complete`.
- The project Log names the round and what was verified.

Summary:
## 1. Primary Request and Intent
The user wants **prompts designed for the Dell laptop** in `20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts - Dell` (currently empty, created Oct 28). They are to be based on the Conversation Loop research and design docs and everything else in `Conversations/`. The goal is to upgrade the Dell's conversation capture and review system to the base layer the Acer is building, and to close the loop so it is running, being used, and improved by practice.

**This pass is research only.** Verbatim: "Do not write anything in this pass, just research in detail through web pages, pdfs, notes, etc. Do not write anything either, just read and research everything in massive detail." The user also said: "You are not going to be relying on the prompts in this phase. This phase is only for research."

The user asked for a detailed read of:
- all the notes and web pages
- the Acer's builds up to the current point (P0 to P4 done, P5 to P8 being implemented there)
- global and in-vault Acer work
- this Dell machine: home directory, current system, and the Windows and WSL conversation scripting

"AC", "ASR" and "ESR" in the dictated text were interpreted as "Acer".

## 2. Key Technical Concepts
- **Conversation Loop layers:**
  - L0 event hook (Node, async, no model, fact rows only)
  - L1 weekly digest (script, ids and links only, rebuilds the current and previous ISO week)
  - L2 second reviewer (stage A Claude-assisted after 30 hand-coded prompts; stage B local model behind a 4-part gate)
  - L3 weekly review (human, about 25 prompts, blind pass, one change, ledger row)
  - L4 monthly review (about 75 minutes)
  - L5 manual `/insights`
- **Per-Prompt Record:** fact rows keyed by `session_id`, `agent`, `prompt_id` (nullable). They hold no prompt text. `claims_done` and `verified_after_last_edit` are computed by the digest, not the hook.
- **Failure Taxonomy:** codes S1 to S7 (from Tang et al.), E1 to E5 (this vault's own) and OK.
- **Intervention Ledger and ten-minute setup review:** one change a week with a metric, baseline and review date. Digest counts per 100 prompts are used for rule-backed codes, and notes for human-coded codes. `inconclusive` is the expected result.
- **Build Plan phases:**
  - P0 to P4 done
  - P4b (The Plan stays local) pending
  - P5 hand review, P6 event hook, P7 digest and P8 schedule and nudge pending
  - P9 watchdog, P10 `30_Order` rewrite, P11 rehearsal, P12 platform and Dell map
  - P13 stage A, P14 Dell rollout, P15 Codex and Cursor, P16 stage B
- **Dell gate:** the Acer loop runs (A1 to A9 true, A10 passed), a written recipe exists, there are no hand copies, scope is fixed to the six repos, and Dell facts have been read.
- **Cross-laptop sync rules (Known Failure Modes):**
  - One git writer (the Acer). The Dell `Jarvis-GitAutoSync` is disabled and its branch is frozen.
  - `.stignore` never syncs, so the Dell's copy is hand-maintained.
  - Per-file ignores need the `(?d)` prefix.
  - Hardcoded paths must be portable (`$PSScriptRoot`, `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`).
  - Registration is per-machine state, so a synced script file does not register itself.
  - A safety net needs a path to a human.
- **Prompt format:** Acer prompts use `<role> <context> <goal> <constraints> <progress_updates> <steps> <verification> <report>` inside a fenced block, plus Run Record and Related Notes headings. The Agent Prompt Standard in `30_Order/Standards/Projects/` instead requires frontmatter `project_kind: prompt`, `task`, `model` and the headings Why This Exists, Hard Rules, Implementation Order, Report Back. This is a conflict to resolve.
- **Claude Code facts verified via web:**
  - 33 hook events.
  - `prompt_id` is absent until the first input.
  - Async hook timeouts are not enforced, and async hooks are killed under `-p`.
  - SessionEnd has a 1.5 s default budget, raised by per-hook `timeout` up to 60 s.
  - `UserPromptSubmit` fires for typed prompts, `/loop`, background-subagent reports and other-session messages. Built-ins like `/clear` are undocumented.
  - `if` is evaluated only on tool events.
  - Hooks are held back until workspace trust is accepted.
  - In PowerShell a bare `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is null; use `$env:`.
  - `usage-data/` is swept and `history.jsonl` is kept.
  - Changelog 2.1.307 to 2.1.318: `SubagentStop` double-firing was fixed in 2.1.311; the emoji-split transcript line was fixed in 2.1.309; `claude -p` silent transcript loss was fixed in 2.1.317; nested `claude -p` reuses the parent session ID unless `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NESTED_SESSION_ID=1`; PowerShell exit-2 handling was fixed in 2.1.316. The 2.1.318 entry is dated Nov 4, which is suspect.
- **Sonnet 5.5 prompting guide:**
  - Effort `medium` for well-specified agentic work and `high` for harder work; reserve `xhigh` and `max`.
  - Carry-through, stop-and-report and real-check paragraphs.
  - Progress updates at set points.
  - Prefill is unsupported, and asking for reasoning in the response invites `reasoning_extraction` declines.
- **Husain and Shankar** (modified 2026-09-01): annotate 30 traces yourself, note the first failure, then an agent may propose more instances, with a pool of about 100.
- **Anthropic research post "How AI agents shape experts' judgment calls"** (Oct 27, 2026; 50 analysts):
  - Analysts matched the agent's lean 86% of the time, and 77% repeated its argument.
  - This directly constrains stage A: flags must stay hidden until after the human's own note.
- **Weekly setup review** (Oct 7, 2026): "what did I correct twice", a single change tested in a fresh session. It is one observed run and the author sells a starter pack.

## 4. Errors and fixes

## 5. Problem Solving
Findings flagged to the user in the final report:
1. The GitHub repo is public, while 46 files of The Plan sessions are tracked and pushed. This is more urgent than P4b's framing, and a history rewrite is the user's decision.
2. Dell transcript retention is 30 days with no snapshot. The 10-06 and 10-07 Windows transcripts expire around 2026-11-05, and the WSL transcripts older than 30 days vanish at the next WSL Claude session start.
3. The Dell's own `The Plan` notes (2) sync to the Acer, where git pushes them. Ignore lines must cover the Dell path.
4. `Jarvis-WeeklyReview` fires on both laptops. A scheduled digest could do the same.
5. The Dell's `Jarvis-Syncthing-Health` shows a false or unexplained alert.
6. The PDF guard on the Dell would point at `mineru-pdf.ps1`, but MinerU is not installed, so it is a dead end.
7. The Dell has no discrete GPU, so stage B (a local model) cannot run on the Dell; the Acer is the stage B host.
8. The Dell's session-continuity hook is not registered, so Dell capture is backfill-only, with no `export_result` outcome log and no event hook.

Ongoing: the Acer's P5 to P8 are not yet run. The sync lag is unknown, but no artifacts exist on this Dell.

## Time-critical items

**WSL transcripts are already past the cutoff.**
- 22 of 42 transcripts (24 of 73 MB) are older than 30 days, and the next WSL Claude session start will delete them.
- WSL's `settings.json` has no `cleanupPeriodDays`, and it is a separate file from the Windows one.
- The rendered notes already exist in the vault. What would be lost is the raw transcripts.

**Dell Windows has until the evening of 2026-11-05.**
- A sweep already ran at 00:35 today.
- The oldest transcripts (10-06 at 20:59 and 21:35) turn 30 days old at those times tomorrow. The next Claude start after that deletes them.
- Neither `cleanupPeriodDays` nor `desktopSessionCleanupPeriodDays` is set.

**The public repo is pushing conversation notes every 30 minutes.**
- `gupta-builds/Jarvis` is public with 0 forks, and the last push was 00:03 today (95 files).
- Exposed on the infra branch: 25 Acer `The Plan` notes (4.6 MB), 21 Acer `Home` notes (1.6 MB), 15 Dell `Home` notes (1.5 MB), 4 Dell `The Plan` notes, 3 Cursor `The Plan` notes, and 131 Cowork notes (5.5 MB).
- **Correction:** my earlier "46 files of The Plan" counted `.done` markers. The real count is 25 Acer notes.
- Neither ignore file has `The Plan`, `Home` or `events-*` lines (`git check-ignore` returns "not ignored" for all four test paths). P4b is not done.
- GitHub's docs say to rotate secrets first, use `git-filter-repo` 2.47 or later, and expect forks and cached views to persist until GitHub Support clears them.

## What the web pass confirmed or corrected

- **Claude Code hooks docs:**
  - 33 events and the 1.5 s SessionEnd budget hold.
  - Plain stdout from `UserPromptSubmit` and `SessionStart` is injected into Claude's context, so the event hook must print nothing.
  - Exec form (`command` plus `args`) avoids quoting problems with the Dell path `C:\Users\Anant Gupta`.
  - Stop input now carries `background_tasks`, `session_crons` and `effort`, which the digest could record.
  - Debug logs show per-hook durations from v2.1.296 on.
- **Retention:**
  - `cleanupPeriodDays: 0` fails validation, and the docs suggest `3650` for long retention.
  - Desktop and Cowork transcripts are kept at any age unless `desktopSessionCleanupPeriodDays` is set, so the Dell Cowork data is not at risk.
  - Changelog items: 2.1.300 added system prompts to local transcripts, 2.1.310 fixed a cleanup bug with very large values, and 2.1.317 fixed missed cleanups.
  - The 2.1.318 changelog entry dated Nov 4 is real, so the earlier doubt is closed.
  - **Correction:** `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NESTED_SESSION_ID` applies to cloud and self-hosted runner sessions, not to local scheduled `claude -p`.
- **Source notes:**
  - Tang v2 matches Failure Taxonomy figure for figure.
  - Gloaguen is still v3.
  - The auto-mode post checks out, including its stop rule of 3 consecutive or 20 total denials.
  - Husain and Shankar and the weekly-review post hold.
- **Still unverified or wrong in the notes:**
  - Hansen's post shows six CLAUDE.md rule blocks, not five, and no re-run.
  - The Every quote was not found, so drop it.
  - Shankar's "high variance" line is not in the text. What the paper says is that random sampling can misalign if the sample is unrepresentative.
  - Dunivin's scope-word range of 0.73 to 0.81 was not located.
- **New source:** the lead the vault couldn't open is at `anthropic.com/research/agents-judgment-calls`, and I read it in full.
  - Analysts followed the agent's lean 86% of the time, and 77% repeated its arguments while 15% credited it.
  - **Analysts who had stated a preference first still switched 60% of the time when the agent disagreed, versus 6% when it agreed** (n=38, CI 24 to 82).
  - So "write your own note first" does not protect on its own. Stage A needs the human's code and note locked before any flag shows, and every code changed after a flag logged with a reason.
  - The study deliberately strengthened the agents' lean and covered one task and one model.

## Other checks

- **Acer progress:** local and remote hashes are identical for every loop note, so there is no sync lag. P5 to P8 had not run as of 00:03, and their Run Records are empty.
- **Syncthing:** `.stignore` never syncs, but it can `#include` a file that does. One hand-applied `#include` line per machine would let future ignore rules sync. A missing included file is an error, and the first matching pattern wins.
- **Codex:** sessions have no expiry, so the Dell's 213 files are safe.

## For the Dell prompts

1. Retention and snapshot comes first, covering Windows and WSL separately, with hard dates.
2. P4b ignore lines must land before any Dell event hook. Otherwise the raw events, which hold prompt text, sync and publish.
3. The Dell hook should use exec form, print nothing to stdout, and coexist with the existing `session-wrapup.ps1`.
4. Stage A must hide flags until the human note is locked, and log changes made after a flag.

=== lifecycle/top
> ## Documentation Index

Assistant
Responses are generated using AI and may contain mistakes.
For a quickstart guide with examples, see [Automate actions with hooks](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide).
Hooks are user-defined shell commands, HTTP endpoints, MCP tool calls, LLM prompts, or subagents that execute automatically at specific points in Claude Code�s lifecycle. Claude Code fires the same hook events wherever it runs: sessions in the terminal, IDE extensions, the [Desktop app](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/desktop-quickstart), and [cloud sessions](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude
## [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#hook-lifecycle)  Hook lifecycle
Claude Code runs hooks at specific points during a session. When an event fires and a matcher matches, Claude Code passes JSON context about the event to your hook handler. For command hooks, input arrives on stdin. For HTTP hooks, it arrives as the POST request body. Your handler can then inspect the input, take action, and optionally return a decision.Events fall into three cadences:
- per session: `SessionStart` and `SessionEnd`
- per turn: `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, and `StopFailure`
- on every tool call inside the agentic loop: `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse`, except [`EndConversation`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/tools-reference#endconversation-tool-behavior) calls, which skip both
![Hook lifecycle diagram showing optional Setup feeding into SessionStart, then a per-turn loop containing UserPromptSubmit, UserPromptExpansion for slash commands, the nested agentic loop (PreToolUse, PermissionRequest, PostToolUse, PostToolUseFailure, PostToolBatch, SubagentStart/Stop, TaskCreated, TaskCompleted), and Stop or StopFailure, followed by TeammateIdle, PreCompact, PostCompact, and Se
The table below summarizes when each event fires. The [Hook events](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#hook-events) section documents the full input schema and decision control options for each one.
| Event | When it fires |
| --- | --- |
| `SessionStart` | When a session begins or resumes |
| `Setup` | When you start Claude Code with `--init-only`, or with `--init` or `--maintenance` in `-p` mode. For one-time preparation in CI or scripts |
| `UserPromptSubmit` | When a prompt is submitted, before Claude processes it. Also fires on [turns Claude Code starts on its own](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#userpromptsubmit) |
| `UserPromptExpansion` | When a user-typed command expands into a prompt, before it reaches Claude. Can block the expansion |
| `PreToolUse` | Before a tool call executes. Can block it |
| `PermissionRequest` | When a tool call needs a permission decision |
| `PermissionDenied` | When auto mode denies a tool call, including denials without a classifier verdict. Use JSON `hookSpecificOutput.retry: true` to tell the model it may retry the denied tool call. Claude Code ignores `retry` when the classifier produced no verdict |
| `PostToolUse` | After a tool call succeeds |
| `PostToolUseFailure` | After a tool call fails |
| `PostToolBatch` | After a full batch of parallel tool calls resolves, before the next model call |
| `Notification` | When Claude Code sends a notification |
| `MessageDisplay` | While assistant message text is displayed |
| `SubagentStart` | When a subagent is spawned |
| `SubagentStop` | When a subagent finishes |
| `TaskCreated` | When a task is being created via `TaskCreate` |
| `TaskCompleted` | When a task is being marked as completed |
| `Stop` | When Claude finishes responding |
| `StopFailure` | When the turn ends due to an API error |
| `TeammateIdle` | When an [agent team](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-teams) teammate is about to go idle |
| `InstructionsLoaded` | When a CLAUDE.md or `.claude/rules/*.md` file is loaded into context. Fires at session start and when files are lazily loaded during a session |
| `ConfigChange` | When a configuration file changes during a session |
| `CwdChanged` | When the working directory changes, for example when Claude executes a `cd` command. Useful for reactive environment management with tools like direnv |
| `DirectoryAdded` | When a working directory is added mid-session via `/add-dir` or the SDK `register_repo_root` control request |
| `FileChanged` | When a watched file changes on disk. The `matcher` field specifies which filenames to watch |
| `WorktreeCreate` | When a worktree is being created via `--worktree`, `isolation: "worktree"`, or for a background session. Replaces default git behavior |
| `WorktreeRemove` | When a worktree that a `WorktreeCreate` hook created is being removed |
| `PreCompact` | Before context compaction |
| `PostCompact` | After context compaction completes |
| `PreModelSwitch` | Before Claude Code applies a model switch that you or a client requested. Can block the switch |
| `PostModelSwitch` | After the session�s model changes, including changes Claude Code makes on its own, such as restoring the model when you resume a session |
| `Elicitation` | When an MCP server requests user input during a tool call |
| `ElicitationResult` | After a user responds to an MCP elicitation, before the response is sent back to the server |
| `SessionEnd` | When a session terminates |
=== common input
## [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#hook-input-and-output)  Hook input and output
Command hooks receive JSON data via stdin and communicate results through exit codes, stdout, and stderr. HTTP hooks receive the same JSON as the POST request body and communicate results through the HTTP response body. This section covers fields and behavior common to all events. Each event�s section under [Hook events](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#hook-events) includes its specific input schema and decision control options.On macOS and Linux, command hooks run in their own session without a controlling terminal. The hook process and any child processes can�t open `/dev/tty` or send 
### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#common-input-fields)  Common input fields
Hook events receive these fields as JSON, in addition to event-specific fields documented in each [hook event](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#hook-events) section. For command hooks, this JSON arrives via stdin. For HTTP hooks, it arrives as the POST request body.
| Field | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `session_id` | Current session identifier |
| `prompt_id` | UUID identifying the user prompt currently being processed. Matches the [`prompt.id` attribute on OpenTelemetry events](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/monitoring-usage#event-correlation-attributes), so you can correlate hook output with telemetry for a single prompt. Absent until the first user input |
| `transcript_path` | Path to conversation JSON. The transcript file is written asynchronously and may lag the in-memory conversation, so it may not yet include the current turn�s most recent messages when a hook fires. Hooks that need the final assistant text of the current turn should use `last_assistant_message` on [Stop](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#stop) and [SubagentStop](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#subagentstop) instead of reading the transcript |
| `cwd` | Current working directory when the hook is invoked |
| `scratchpad_dir` | Path to the session�s [scratchpad directory](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-directory#session-scratchpad-directory), where Claude keeps temporary working files. Absent when the session has no scratchpad or the temp directory is unavailable. Requires Claude Code v2.1.257 or later |
| `permission_mode` | Current [permission mode](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions#permission-modes): `"default"`, `"plan"`, `"acceptEdits"`, `"auto"`, `"dontAsk"`, or `"bypassPermissions"`. The mode labeled **Manual** arrives as `"default"`, never as `"manual"`, so scripts that match `"default"` keep working. Not all events receive this field. Check the JSON example in each [hook event](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#hook-events) section |
| `effort` | Object whose `level` is the [effort level](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config#adjust-effort-level) setting when the hook runs, including a skill�s, subagent�s, or `ultrathink` level: `"low"`, `"medium"`, `"high"`, `"xhigh"`, or `"max"`. Present for `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `Stop`, `SubagentStop`, and other events with a tool-use context, on models with effort. If the model doesn�t support your level, `level` is the level that ran. A level you changed can show here before it [starts](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config#when-a-new-effort-level-starts). `$CLAUD
| `hook_event_name` | Name of the event that fired |
`agent_id` and `agent_type` tell your script which agent a hook fired in, such as a subagent, an [in-process teammate](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-teams#choose-a-display-mode), or the agent you chose with `--agent`:
| Field | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `agent_id` | Unique identifier for the subagent or in-process teammate the hook fires in. |
| `agent_type` | Agent name (for example, `"Explore"` or `"security-reviewer"`). Present when the session uses `--agent` or the hook fires inside a subagent. For subagents, the subagent�s type takes precedence over the session�s `--agent` value. See [SubagentStart](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#subagentstart) for the values custom and plugin subagents report and how to write a matcher against a plugin-scoped name. |
Only [`SessionStart`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#sessionstart) hooks can receive a `model` field, and Claude Code doesn�t always include it. [`PreModelSwitch`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#premodelswitch) and [`PostModelSwitch`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#postmodelswitch) hooks receive `from_model` and `to_model` instead, so use a PostModelSwitch hook to follow the model as it changes during a session.There is no `$CLAUDE_MODEL` environment variable. The hook can read `$ANTHROPIC_MODEL` if you set it in your shell, but that value doesn�t change when you switch mo
```
{
  "session_id": "abc123",
  "prompt_id": "[REDACTED]",
  "transcript_path": "/home/user/.claude/projects/.../transcript.jsonl",
  "cwd": "/home/user/my-project",
  "scratchpad_dir": "/tmp/claude-1000/-home-user-my-project/abc123/scratchpad",
  "permission_mode": "default",
  "hook_event_name": "PreToolUse",
  "tool_name": "Bash",
  "tool_input": {
    "command": "npm test",
    "description": "Run test suite",
    "timeout": 120000,
    "run_in_background": false
  },
  "tool_use_id": "toolu_01ABC123..."
}
```
The `tool_name`, `tool_input`, and `tool_use_id` fields are event-specific. Each [hook event](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#hook-events) section documents the additional fields for that event.

=== UserPromptSubmit
### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#userpromptsubmit)  UserPromptSubmit
Runs when a prompt is submitted, before Claude processes it. This allows you
to add additional context based on the prompt/conversation, validate prompts, or
block certain types of prompts.`UserPromptSubmit` hooks don�t fire only on prompts you type. Claude Code also runs them on:
- A [session-scoped scheduled task](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/scheduled-tasks) firing, including a `/loop` iteration
- A [background subagent](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents#run-subagents-in-foreground-or-background) reporting back to the session that started it
- A [message another session sends](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cross-session-messaging) to your main conversation
`UserPromptSubmit` hooks have a default timeout of 30 seconds for `command`, `http`, and `mcp_tool` types, shorter than the 600-second default for those types on most other events. Because this hook runs before every prompt and blocks model processing until it completes, a stuck hook stalls the session. If your hook needs more time, set the `timeout` field in the hook entry.Apart from a command hook you run with [`async: true`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#run-hooks-in-the-background), a `UserPromptSubmit` command, HTTP, or MCP tool hook that reaches its timeout is canceled and its output, including any `additionalContext`, is discarded. The prompt still reaches Claude without that context. To block the prompt instead, set [`onFailure: "block"`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#block-the-action-when-a-hook-fails) on a command or HTTP hook. The transcript shows a notice nami
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#esc-during-a-prompt-check)  `Esc` during a prompt check
If you press `Esc` or interrupt the session while hooks are still checking a prompt, the check stops and the prompt doesn�t reach Claude. Text you typed at the terminal returns to the prompt input, and the hooks check it again when you resend it.A hook can block the prompt at the same moment you interrupt. The prompt then stays blocked: you see that hook�s [block message](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#what-a-blocked-prompt-leaves-behind), and the text doesn�t return to the input.
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#userpromptsubmit-input)  UserPromptSubmit input
In addition to the [common input fields](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#common-input-fields), UserPromptSubmit hooks receive the `prompt` field containing the submitted text. Pasted content that collapsed to a `[Pasted text #N]` placeholder arrives expanded in place. In sessions where Claude Code [marks pasted text for Claude](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/terminal-config#how-claude-treats-pasted-text), that expanded content sits between a `<pasted_content id="�">` line and a `</pasted_content id="�">` line, so account for those lines if your hook parses the prompt.UserPromptSubmit hooks also receive `session_title` when the session has a custom title, with the same meaning as the [SessionStart `session_title` field](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#sessionstart-input).
```
{
  "session_id": "abc123",
  "transcript_path": "/Users/.../.claude/projects/.../[REDACTED].jsonl",
  "cwd": "/Users/...",
  "permission_mode": "default",
  "hook_event_name": "UserPromptSubmit",
  "prompt": "Write a function to calculate the factorial of a number"
}
```
When the prompt is a [skill](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills) or custom command you typed, such as `/deploy staging`, the hook receives that line as you typed it rather than the expanded skill content. If the hook blocks the prompt, the command doesn�t run, so shell commands the skill [injects with ``!`<command>```](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills#inject-dynamic-context) don�t run either. To match on the command�s name or arguments instead, use [UserPromptExpansion](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#userpromptexpansion).
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#userpromptsubmit-decision-control)  UserPromptSubmit decision control
`UserPromptSubmit` hooks can control whether a submitted prompt is processed and add context. All [JSON output fields](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#json-output) are available.There are two ways to add context to the conversation on exit code 0:
- **Plain text stdout**: Claude Code adds stdout it [treats as plain text](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#exit-code-0) to Claude�s context
- **JSON with `additionalContext`**: use the JSON format below for more control. The `additionalContext` field is added as context
Neither channel produces a visible transcript entry. Plain stdout and the `additionalContext` value are each injected as a system reminder that starts with the hook�s name; Claude reads both. To confirm delivery, check the [debug log](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#debug-hooks).To block a prompt, return a JSON object with `decision` set to `"block"`:
| Field | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `decision` | `"block"` stops the prompt before it reaches Claude. Omit to allow the prompt to proceed |
| `reason` | Shown to the user when `decision` is `"block"`. Not added to context |
| `additionalContext` | String added to Claude�s context alongside the submitted prompt. See [Add context for Claude](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#add-context-for-claude) |
| `sessionTitle` | Sets the session title. Use to name sessions automatically based on the prompt content |
| `suppressOriginalPrompt` | If `true` when the hook blocks the prompt, leaves the prompt text out of the block message. See [What a blocked prompt leaves behind](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#what-a-blocked-prompt-leaves-behind) |
A hook that blocks by exiting 2 routes the same way as `reason`: the block message shows the stderr text to the user, and it isn�t added to context.
```
{
  "decision": "block",
  "reason": "Explanation for decision",
  "hookSpecificOutput": {
    "hookEventName": "UserPromptSubmit",
    "additionalContext": "My additional context here",
    "sessionTitle": "My session title",
    "suppressOriginalPrompt": true
  }
}
```
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#what-a-blocked-prompt-leaves-behind)  What a blocked prompt leaves behind
A blocked prompt never reaches Claude, but its text isn�t removed everywhere. By default the block message shown to the user ends with `Original prompt:` followed by the submitted text, and Claude Code writes that message to the session�s transcript file on disk. To leave the text out of the message, print JSON with `"suppressOriginalPrompt": true` inside `hookSpecificOutput`. This works whether the hook blocks with `decision: "block"` or by exiting 2.`suppressOriginalPrompt` changes only the block message. The submitted text can still appear in local files such as the session transcript and your prompt history, so a blocking hook isn�t a way to keep a secret off disk. To limit or remove those files, see [Plaintext storage](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-directory#plaintext-storage) and [Clear local data](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-directory#clear-local-data).
### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#userpromptexpansion)  UserPromptExpansion
Runs when a user-typed command expands into a prompt before reaching Claude. Use this to block specific commands from direct invocation, inject context for a particular skill, or log which commands users invoke. For example, a hook matching `deploy` can block `/deploy` unless an approval file is present, or a hook matching a review skill can append the team�s review checklist as `additionalContext`.This event covers the path `PreToolUse` doesn�t: a `PreToolUse` hook matching the `Skill` tool fires only when Claude calls the tool, but typing `/skillname` directly bypasses `PreToolUse`. `UserPromptExpansion` fires on that direct path.Matches on `command_name`. Leave the matcher empty to fire on every prompt-type command.
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#userpromptexpansion-input)  UserPromptExpansion input
In addition to the [common input fields](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#common-input-fields), UserPromptExpansion hooks receive `expansion_type`, `command_name`, `command_args`, `command_source`, and the original `prompt` string. The `expansion_type` field is `slash_command` for skill and custom commands, or `mcp_prompt` for MCP server prompts.
```
{
  "session_id": "abc123",
  "transcript_path": "/Users/.../00893aaf.jsonl",
  "cwd": "/Users/...",
  "permission_mode": "default",
  "hook_event_name": "UserPromptExpansion",
  "expansion_type": "slash_command",
  "command_name": "example-skill",
  "command_args": "arg1 arg2",
  "command_source": "plugin",
  "prompt": "/example-skill arg1 arg2"
}
```
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#userpromptexpansion-decision-control)  UserPromptExpansion decision control
`UserPromptExpansion` hooks can block the expansion or add context. All [JSON output fields](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#json-output) are available.
| Field | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `decision` | `"block"` prevents the command from expanding. Omit to allow it to proceed |
| `reason` | Shown to the user when `decision` is `"block"` |
| `additionalContext` | String added to Claude�s context alongside the expanded prompt. See [Add context for Claude](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#add-context-for-claude) |
A hook that blocks by exiting 2 routes the same way as `reason`: the block message shows the stderr text to the user.
```
{
  "decision": "block",
  "reason": "This slash command is not available",
  "hookSpecificOutput": {
    "hookEventName": "UserPromptExpansion",
    "additionalContext": "Additional context for this expansion"
  }
}
```
=== Stop
### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#stop)  Stop
Runs when the main Claude Code agent has finished responding. Does not run if
the stoppage occurred due to a user interrupt. API errors fire
[StopFailure](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#stopfailure) instead.
The [`/goal`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/goal) command is a built-in shortcut for a session-scoped prompt-based Stop hook. Use it when you want Claude to keep working toward a condition without writing hook configuration.
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#stop-input)  Stop input
In addition to the [common input fields](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#common-input-fields), Stop hooks receive `stop_hook_active`, `last_assistant_message`, `background_tasks`, and `session_crons`. The `stop_hook_active` field is `true` when Claude Code is already continuing as a result of a stop hook. Check this value or process the transcript to avoid blocking on a condition that will never resolve.Claude Code applies an [REDACTED] cap: after stop hooks have continued the turn eight times in a row, Claude Code overrides the next block and ends the turn. The count of consecutive continuations resets each time Claude calls a tool. To raise the cap, set [`CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars).The `last_assistant_message` field contains the text content of Claude�s final response, so hooks can access it without parsing the tran
| Field | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `id` | Task identifier |
| `type` | Friendly task-type label such as `shell`, `subagent`, `monitor`, `workflow`, `teammate`, `cloud session`, or `MCP task`. Each label identifies which Claude Code feature created the task. Falls back to the raw discriminant for unrecognized types |
| `status` | Current task status |
| `description` | Free-text description, capped at 1000 characters with an in-string `� [+N chars]` marker when clipped |
| `command` | Shell command line, capped at 1000 characters. Present only for `shell` tasks |
| `agent_type` | Subagent type name. Present only for `subagent` tasks |
| `server` | MCP server name. Present only for `monitor` and `MCP task` tasks |
| `tool` | MCP tool name. Present only for `monitor` and `MCP task` tasks |
| `name` | Workflow name. Present only for `workflow` tasks |
Each entry in `session_crons` describes one session-scoped scheduled wakeup, sourced from `CronCreate`, `ScheduleWakeup`, and `/loop`:
| Field | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `id` | Cron task identifier |
| `schedule` | Cron expression, for example `0 9 * * 1-5` |
| `recurring` | `false` for one-shot wakeups whose schedule encodes a single fire time, `true` for tasks that re-fire on every match |
| `prompt` | Prompt submitted when the cron fires, capped at 1000 characters with the same `� [+N chars]` marker |
This example shows a Stop input with one in-flight shell task and one recurring cron:
```
{
  "session_id": "abc123",
  "transcript_path": "~/.claude/projects/.../[REDACTED].jsonl",
  "cwd": "/Users/...",
  "permission_mode": "default",
  "hook_event_name": "Stop",
  "stop_hook_active": true,
  "last_assistant_message": "I've completed the refactoring. Here's a summary...",
  "background_tasks": [\
    {\
      "id": "task-001",\
      "type": "shell",\
      "status": "running",\
      "description": "tail logs",\
      "command": "tail -f /var/log/syslog"\
    }\
  ],
  "session_crons": [\
    {\
      "id": "cron-001",\
      "schedule": "0 9 * * 1-5",\
      "recurring": true,\
      "prompt": "check the build"\
    }\
  ]
}
```
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#stop-decision-control)  Stop decision control
`Stop` and `SubagentStop` hooks can control whether Claude continues. In addition to the [JSON output fields](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#json-output) available to all hooks, your hook script can return these event-specific fields:
| Field | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `decision` | `"block"` prevents Claude from stopping. Omit to allow Claude to stop |
| `reason` | Required when `decision` is `"block"`. Tells Claude why it should continue |
| `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` | Non-error feedback for Claude. The conversation continues so Claude can act on it, but unlike `decision: "block"` it is shown in the transcript as hook feedback rather than a hook error |
A hook that blocks by exiting 2 routes the same way as `reason`: Claude receives the stderr message as the explanation for why it should continue.
```
{
  "decision": "block",
  "reason": "Must be provided when Claude is blocked from stopping"
}
```
Use `additionalContext` when the hook is working as designed and giving Claude guidance, such as �run the test suite before finishing�. It keeps the conversation going through the same loop protections as `decision: "block"`, namely the `stop_hook_active` input and the [REDACTED] cap, but the transcript labels it `Stop hook feedback` and no hook error notification is shown:
```
{
  "hookSpecificOutput": {
    "hookEventName": "Stop",
    "additionalContext": "Please run the test suite before finishing"
  }
}
```
### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#stopfailure)  StopFailure
Runs instead of [Stop](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#stop) when the turn ends due to an API error. Claude Code ignores the hook�s output and exit code, apart from [`terminalSequence`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#emit-terminal-notifications). Use this to log failures, send alerts, or take recovery actions when Claude can�t complete a response due to rate limits, authentication problems, or other API errors.
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#stopfailure-input)  StopFailure input
In addition to the [common input fields](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#common-input-fields), StopFailure hooks receive `error`, optional `error_details`, and optional `last_assistant_message`. The `error` field identifies the error type and is used for matcher filtering.
| Field | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `error` | Error type: `rate_limit`, `overloaded`, `authentication_failed`, `oauth_org_not_allowed`, `account_on_hold`, `billing_error`, `invalid_request`, `model_not_found`, `server_error`, `max_output_tokens`, `cloud_credential_error`, or `unknown` |
| `error_details` | Additional details about the error, when available |
| `last_assistant_message` | The rendered error text shown in the conversation. Unlike `Stop` and `SubagentStop`, where this field holds Claude�s conversational output, for `StopFailure` it contains the API error string itself, such as `"API Error: Rate limit reached"` |
```
{
  "session_id": "abc123",
  "transcript_path": "/Users/.../.claude/projects/.../[REDACTED].jsonl",
  "cwd": "/Users/...",
  "hook_event_name": "StopFailure",
  "error": "rate_limit",
  "error_details": "429 Too Many Requests",
  "last_assistant_message": "API Error: Rate limit reached"
}
```
StopFailure hooks have no decision control. They run for notification and logging purposes only.
=== SessionEnd
### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#sessionend)  SessionEnd
Runs when a Claude Code session ends. Useful for cleanup tasks, logging session
statistics, or saving session state. Supports matchers to filter by exit reason.The `reason` field in the hook input indicates why the session ended:
| Reason | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `clear` | Session cleared with `/clear` command |
| `resume` | Session switched via interactive `/resume` |
| `logout` | User logged out |
| `prompt_input_exit` | User exited while prompt input was visible |
| `other` | Other exit reasons |
| `bypass_permissions_disabled` | Removed in v2.1.234; Claude Code doesn�t send it. Drop it from your `SessionEnd` matchers |
#### [](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks\#sessionend-input)  SessionEnd input
In addition to the [common input fields](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#common-input-fields), SessionEnd hooks receive a `reason` field indicating why the session ended. See the [reason table](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#sessionend) above for all values.
```
{
  "session_id": "abc123",
  "transcript_path": "/Users/.../.claude/projects/.../[REDACTED].jsonl",
  "cwd": "/Users/...",
  "hook_event_name": "SessionEnd",
  "reason": "other"
}
```
SessionEnd hooks have no decision control. They can�t block session termination but can perform cleanup tasks. Claude Code discards their [JSON output fields](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#json-output), such as `systemMessage`.SessionEnd hooks have a default timeout of 1.5 seconds. It applies when you exit, run `/clear`, or switch sessions with interactive `/resume`. You can give a hook more time in two ways:
- **Per-hook `timeout`**: set `timeout` in that hook�s configuration. The overall budget rises automatically to match the highest per-hook `timeout` in your settings files, up to 60 seconds. If you raise the budget this way, a hook without its own `timeout` still keeps the default. Timeouts set on plugin-provided hooks don�t raise the budget.
- **`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSIONEND_HOOKS_TIMEOUT_MS`**: set this environment variable in milliseconds to override the budget explicitly. The value you set also becomes the timeout for each hook without its own `timeout`.
This example sets the budget to 5 seconds:
```
CLAUDE_CODE_SESSIONEND_HOOKS_TIMEOUT_MS=5000 claude
```
Before v2.1.268, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSIONEND_HOOKS_TIMEOUT_MS` raised only the overall budget, and a hook without its own `timeout` was still canceled after 1.5 seconds.
